`Crackmes_and_CTF/01_Apprentice_Gate/crackme01_strings.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 01] The Apprentice Gate: Strings & Stack Strings (Tier F+ | 0.7/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   1. Finding plaintext strings in the `.rdata` section using `strings`,
//      Ghidra, or x64dbg ("Search for -> All Referenced Text Strings").
//   2. Understanding why "Stack Strings" (characters pushed onto the stack one
//      by one at runtime) hide from basic `strings` scans, and how to read them
//      in a decompiler or inspect the memory buffer at a breakpoint!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <string>
#include <cstring>

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 01 | TIER F+] THE APPRENTICE GATE (Strings & Stack)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";

    // Stage 1: Classic .rdata string comparison
    std::cout << "[Stage 1/2] Enter Apprentice Passcode: ";
    std::string input1;
    std::cin >> input1;

    if (input1 != "OPEN_SESAME_x86") {
        std::cout << ">> [ACCESS DENIED] Wrong Stage 1 passcode! (Tip: Check Referenced Strings in x64dbg/Ghidra)\n";
        return 1;
    }
    std::cout << ">> [STAGE 1 CLEARED] Good! Now let's see if you can find a Stack String...\n\n";

    // Stage 2: Stack-constructed string (Does NOT appear as a contiguous string in .rdata!)
    volatile char stackSecret[14];
    stackSecret[0]  = 'S';
    stackSecret[1]  = 'T';
    stackSecret[2]  = 'A';
    stackSecret[3]  = 'C';
    stackSecret[4]  = 'K';
    stackSecret[5]  = '_';
    stackSecret[6]  = 'A';
    stackSecret[7]  = 'L';
    stackSecret[8]  = 'C';
    stackSecret[9]  = 'H';
    stackSecret[10] = 'E';
    stackSecret[11] = 'M';
    stackSecret[12] = 'Y';
    stackSecret[13] = '\0';

    std::cout << "[Stage 2/2] Enter Stack Alchemist Key: ";
    std::string input2;
    std::cin >> input2;

    bool match = (input2.size() == 13);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < 13 && match; ++i) {
        if (input2[i] != stackSecret[i]) match = false;
    }

    if (match) {
        std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
        std::cout << "  [CRACKME 01 SOLVED - TIER F+ CLEARED!]\n";
        std::cout << "  Flag: FLAG{STACK_STRINGS_CANNOT_HIDE_FROM_GHIDRA}\n";
        std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 02 (Mathematical Serial Keygenme).\n";
        std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
        return 0;
    } else {
        std::cout << ">> [ACCESS DENIED] Wrong Stage 2 key! Inspect the stack frame in Ghidra/x64dbg!\n";
        return 1;
    }
}

```

`Crackmes_and_CTF/02_Mathematical_Lock/crackme02_serial_math.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 02] The Mathematical Lock: Keygenme (Tier E | Difficulty: 1.9/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   Builds on Crackme 01. Instead of comparing against a hardcoded string, real
//   software serial checks verify mathematical relationships between blocks of
//   your serial key (`AAAA-BBBB-CCCC`).
//   Reverse the arithmetic in Ghidra/IDA and write a Keygen script that can
//   generate valid serials!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdint>

#if defined(_MSC_VER)
#define NOINLINE __declspec(noinline)
#else
#define NOINLINE __attribute__((noinline))
#endif

NOINLINE bool ValidateSerialKey(int a, int b, int c) {
    // 1. Range check: each block must be a 4-digit number (1000..9999)
    if (a < 1000 || a > 9999 || b < 1000 || b > 9999 || c < 1000 || c > 9999) {
        return false;
    }
    // 2. Constraint 1: Sum of Block A and Block B must equal 10000
    if ((a + b) != 10000) {
        return false;
    }
    // 3. Constraint 2: Bitwise XOR of Block A and Block B must equal 9984 (0x2700)
    //    (e.g. A = 1664, B = 8336 -> 1664 + 8336 = 10000, 1664 ^ 8336 = 9984)
    if ((a ^ b) != 9984) {
        return false;
    }
    // 4. Constraint 3: Block C is derived from A and B
    int expectedC = ((a * 3) + (b ^ 0x1234)) % 9000 + 1000;
    return (c == expectedC);
}

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 02 | TIER E] THE MATHEMATICAL LOCK (Serial Keygenme)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "Enter Product Serial Key (Format: AAAA-BBBB-CCCC): ";

    int a = 0, b = 0, c = 0;
    if (std::scanf("%d-%d-%d", &a, &b, &c) != 3) {
        std::cout << ">> [INVALID FORMAT] Please use 4-digit blocks: 1234-5678-9012\n";
        return 1;
    }

    if (ValidateSerialKey(a, b, c)) {
        std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
        std::cout << "  [CRACKME 02 SOLVED - TIER E CLEARED!]\n";
        std::cout << "  Valid Serial Accepted: " << a << "-" << b << "-" << c << "\n";
        std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 03 (XOR & Bit-Rotation Cipher).\n";
        std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
        return 0;
    } else {
        std::cout << ">> [SERIAL REJECTED] Analyze ValidateSerialKey() in Ghidra and write a Keygen!\n";
        return 1;
    }
}

```

`Crackmes_and_CTF/03_XOR_and_Rol_Cipher/crackme03_xor_rol.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 03] XOR & Bitwise Rotation Cipher (Tier D | Difficulty: 3.1/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   Builds on Crackme 02. Teaches how to recognize bitwise rotation idioms
//   (`ROL` / `ROR`: `(x << n) | (x >> (8 - n))`) and position-dependent XOR
//   keys in disassembled loops, and how to invert the transformation in Python.
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <string>
#include <cstdint>

static inline uint8_t Rol8(uint8_t val, int shift) {
    shift &= 7;
    return static_cast<uint8_t>((val << shift) | (val >> (8 - shift)));
}

// Target encrypted bytes corresponding to the 20-character ASCII flag:
// "BITWISE_ALCHEMIST_99"
// Formula per index i: target[i] = Rol8(input[i] ^ ((0x5A + i * 7) & 0xFF), 3)
static const uint8_t kEncryptedTarget[20] = {
    0xC0, 0x41, 0xE1, 0xC1, 0xF9, 0x71, 0x0E, 0xA6, 0x9E, 0xAE,
    0x1F, 0x7F, 0x5F, 0xC7, 0xAF, 0x84, 0xF4, 0x74, 0x0F, 0x37
};

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 03 | TIER D] THE ALCHEMIST'S CIPHER (XOR + ROL8 Loop)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "Enter the 20-character Decryption Rune: ";

    std::string input;
    std::cin >> input;

    if (input.size() != 20) {
        std::cout << ">> [FAILED] Input must be exactly 20 characters long!\n";
        return 1;
    }

    for (size_t i = 0; i < 20; ++i) {
        uint8_t keyByte = static_cast<uint8_t>(0x5A + i * 7);
        uint8_t xored   = static_cast<uint8_t>(input[i]) ^ keyByte;
        uint8_t rotated = Rol8(xored, 3);
        if (rotated != kEncryptedTarget[i]) {
            std::cout << ">> [FAILED] Mismatch at byte index " << i
                      << "! Reverse the Rol8 + XOR loop in Ghidra!\n";
            return 1;
        }
    }

    std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 03 SOLVED - TIER D CLEARED!]\n";
    std::cout << "  Decrypted Rune Verified: FLAG{" << input << "}\n";
    std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 04 (Binary License File Format RE).\n";
    std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
    return 0;
}

```

`Crackmes_and_CTF/04_Struct_License_File/crackme04_binary_license.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 04] Binary File Format & Struct Auditing (Tier C | Diff: 4.3/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   Builds on Crackme 03. Many desktop programs (WinRAR, Sublime, game saves,
//   custom archives) validate a binary file on disk rather than a text string.
//   You will reverse engineer the exact byte layout of `license.dat` (a packed
//   24-byte C structure) and write a Python generator using `struct.pack`!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <cstdint>
#include <iomanip>

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct BinaryLicenseHeader {
    uint32_t magic;        // +0x00: Must be 0x56414544 ("DEAV" in little-endian)
    uint16_t version;      // +0x04: Must be 0x0104 (v1.4)
    uint16_t tierBadge;    // +0x06: Must be 0x0777
    uint64_t citizenId;    // +0x08: Must be >= 0x00000000000186A0 (100,000)
    uint32_t featureFlags; // +0x10: Must have bits 0x80000000 and 0x0000002A set
    uint32_t crcTag;       // +0x14: Must equal (magic ^ (uint32_t)citizenId ^ featureFlags ^ 0xA5A5A5A5)
};
#pragma pack(pop)

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 04 | TIER C] THE ROYAL ARCHIVE (Binary File Format RE)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "Looking for binary license file: ./license.dat (24 bytes)...\n";

    std::ifstream file("license.dat", std::ios::binary | std::ios::ate);
    if (!file.is_open()) {
        std::cout << ">> [ERROR] `license.dat` not found in current directory!\n";
        std::cout << "   Disassemble this binary in Ghidra to reconstruct the 24-byte\n";
        std::cout << "   `BinaryLicenseHeader` struct and craft `license.dat` with Python!\n";
        return 1;
    }

    std::streamsize size = file.tellg();
    if (size != sizeof(BinaryLicenseHeader)) {
        std::cout << ">> [INVALID SIZE] Expected exactly " << sizeof(BinaryLicenseHeader)
                  << " bytes, got " << size << " bytes.\n";
        return 1;
    }

    file.seekg(0, std::ios::beg);
    BinaryLicenseHeader hdr{};
    file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&hdr), sizeof(hdr));

    if (hdr.magic != 0x56414544U) {
        std::cout << ">> [CHECK 1 FAILED] Invalid Magic Header (0x" << std::hex << hdr.magic << ")!\n";
        return 1;
    }
    if (hdr.version != 0x0104U || hdr.tierBadge != 0x0777U) {
        std::cout << ">> [CHECK 2 FAILED] Invalid Version or TierBadge field!\n";
        return 1;
    }
    if (hdr.citizenId < 100000ULL) {
        std::cout << ">> [CHECK 3 FAILED] CitizenId must be >= 100,000!\n";
        return 1;
    }
    const uint32_t reqBits = 0x8000002AU;
    if ((hdr.featureFlags & reqBits) != reqBits) {
        std::cout << ">> [CHECK 4 FAILED] Required FeatureFlags bits are not set!\n";
        return 1;
    }
    uint32_t expectedCrc = hdr.magic ^ static_cast<uint32_t>(hdr.citizenId & 0xFFFFFFFFULL)
                         ^ hdr.featureFlags ^ 0xA5A5A5A5U;
    if (hdr.crcTag != expectedCrc) {
        std::cout << ">> [CHECK 5 FAILED] Checksum mismatch! Expected 0x"
                  << std::hex << expectedCrc << ", got 0x" << hdr.crcTag << std::dec << "\n";
        return 1;
    }

    std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 04 SOLVED - TIER C CLEARED!]\n";
    std::cout << "  Valid `license.dat` forged for Citizen ID #" << hdr.citizenId << "!\n";
    std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 05 (User-Mode Anti-Debugging Sentinel).\n";
    std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
    return 0;
}

```

`Crackmes_and_CTF/05_AntiDebug_Sentinel/crackme05_antidebug.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 05] Anti-Debug Sentinel & FNV-1a Key Check (Tier B | Diff: 5.5/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   Builds on Crackme 04. Commercial binaries and crackmes actively fight back
//   against debuggers! This target implements 3 classic anti-debug checks:
//     1. Win32 `IsDebuggerPresent()` API check (reads `PEB->BeingDebugged`).
//     2. `CheckRemoteDebuggerPresent()` API check.
//     3. Software Breakpoint (`0xCC` / `INT3`) prolog scanner on `VerifyKey()`.
//
//   Learn how to bypass these using ScyllaHide, patching `PEB->BeingDebugged`,
//   or using Hardware Breakpoints (`DR0`-`DR3`) instead of `0xCC`!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <string>
#include <cstdint>
#ifdef _WIN32
#include <windows.h>
#endif

#if defined(_MSC_VER)
#define NOINLINE __declspec(noinline)
#else
#define NOINLINE __attribute__((noinline))
#endif

NOINLINE bool VerifyKeyFNV1a(const std::string& key) {
    // Computes 32-bit FNV-1a hash of the input string
    // Target phrase: "SHADOW_DEBUGGER_SLAYER" -> FNV-1a = 0x7A4B0C29
    uint32_t hash = 2166136261U;
    for (unsigned char c : key) {
        hash ^= c;
        hash *= 16777619U;
    }
    return (hash == 0x7A4B0C29U) && (key.size() == 22) && (key[0] == 'S') && (key[6] == '_');
}

NOINLINE bool DetectDebuggerTrap() {
#ifdef _WIN32
    if (IsDebuggerPresent()) {
        std::cout << ">> [ANTI-DEBUG #1] IsDebuggerPresent() detected a debugger (PEB->BeingDebugged == 1)!\n";
        return true;
    }
    BOOL remoteDbg = FALSE;
    CheckRemoteDebuggerPresent(GetCurrentProcess(), &remoteDbg);
    if (remoteDbg) {
        std::cout << ">> [ANTI-DEBUG #2] CheckRemoteDebuggerPresent() triggered!\n";
        return true;
    }
#endif
    // Anti-Debug #3: Scan the first 24 bytes of VerifyKeyFNV1a for 0xCC (INT3 software breakpoint)
    const uint8_t* fnBytes = reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(&VerifyKeyFNV1a);
    for (int i = 0; i < 24; ++i) {
        if (fnBytes[i] == 0xCC) {
            std::cout << ">> [ANTI-DEBUG #3] Software Breakpoint (0xCC / INT3) detected inside VerifyKeyFNV1a()!\n";
            std::cout << "   Tip: Use Hardware Breakpoints (DR0-DR3) instead of F2 Software Breakpoints!\n";
            return true;
        }
    }
    return false;
}

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 05 | TIER B] THE ANTI-DEBUG SENTINEL (PEB & INT3 Scan)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";

    if (DetectDebuggerTrap()) {
        std::cout << ">> [TERMINATED] Bypass the Anti-Debug checks in x64dbg (or enable ScyllaHide)!\n";
        return 1;
    }

    std::cout << ">> [INTEGRITY OK] No active debugger traps tripped.\n";
    std::cout << "Enter Sentinel Master Key: ";
    std::string key;
    std::cin >> key;

    if (DetectDebuggerTrap()) {
        return 1;
    }

    if (VerifyKeyFNV1a(key)) {
        std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
        std::cout << "  [CRACKME 05 SOLVED - TIER B CLEARED!]\n";
        std::cout << "  You bypassed the Anti-Debug Sentinel! Key: " << key << "\n";
        std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 06 (Custom Bytecode Stack VM).\n";
        std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
        return 0;
    } else {
        std::cout << ">> [INVALID KEY] Sentinel rejected your input.\n";
        return 1;
    }
}

`Crackmes_and_CTF/06_Custom_Stack_VM/crackme06_bytecode_vm.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 06] Custom Bytecode Stack Virtual Machine (Tier A | Diff: 6.8/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   Builds on Crackme 05. How do VMProtect, Themida, and game scripting VMs
//   (LuaJIT, QuakeC, GameMaker) work? Instead of compiling logic to native
//   x86_64 instructions, the logic is compiled into custom VM bytecode executed
//   by a Virtual Machine interpreter loop (`while (running) { switch(op) ... }`)!
//
// QUEST OBJECTIVE:
//   1. Locate the `VM_Interpreter` opcode table and `g_BytecodeProgram[]`.
//   2. Write a small Python disassembler (or use the starter script in
//      `Tools_and_Scripts/04_Python_RE_Toolkit`) to recover the 8-char password!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstdint>

enum VmOpcode : uint8_t {
    OP_PUSH_IMM   = 0x01, // Push next byte onto VM stack
    OP_PUSH_INPUT = 0x02, // Push user input[imm] onto VM stack
    OP_ADD        = 0x10, // Pop A, Pop B -> Push (B + A) & 0xFF
    OP_XOR        = 0x11, // Pop A, Pop B -> Push (B ^ A) & 0xFF
    OP_ROL3       = 0x12, // Pop A -> Push ROL8(A, 3)
    OP_ASSERT_EQ  = 0x20, // Pop A, Pop B -> If A != B, fail VM!
    OP_HALT_WIN   = 0xFF  // All checks passed!
};

static inline uint8_t VmRol3(uint8_t v) {
    return static_cast<uint8_t>((v << 3) | (v >> 5));
}

// Each 7-byte block validates 1 character of the 8-byte secret key ("VM_KING!")
// Transform: ((input[i] ^ key1) + key2) & 0xFF == expected
static const uint8_t g_BytecodeProgram[] = {
    // Char 0: ('V' ^ 0x12) + 0x05 == (0x56 ^ 0x12) + 5 = 0x44 + 5 = 0x49
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x00, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x12, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x05, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x49, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 1: ('M' ^ 0x23) + 0x07 == (0x4D ^ 0x23) + 7 = 0x6E + 7 = 0x75
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x01, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x23, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x07, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x75, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 2: ('_' ^ 0x34) + 0x09 == (0x5F ^ 0x34) + 9 = 0x6B + 9 = 0x74
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x02, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x34, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x09, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x74, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 3: ('K' ^ 0x45) + 0x0B == (0x4B ^ 0x45) + 11 = 0x0E + 11 = 0x19
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x03, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x45, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x0B, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x19, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 4: ('I' ^ 0x56) + 0x0D == (0x49 ^ 0x56) + 13 = 0x1F + 13 = 0x2C
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x04, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x56, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x0D, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x2C, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 5: ('N' ^ 0x67) + 0x0F == (0x4E ^ 0x67) + 15 = 0x29 + 15 = 0x38
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x05, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x67, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x0F, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x38, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 6: ('G' ^ 0x78) + 0x11 == (0x47 ^ 0x78) + 17 = 0x3F + 17 = 0x50
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x06, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x78, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x11, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x50, OP_ASSERT_EQ,
    // Char 7: ('!' ^ 0x19) + 0x03 == (0x21 ^ 0x19) + 3 = 0x38 + 3 = 0x3B
    OP_PUSH_INPUT, 0x07, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x19, OP_XOR, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x03, OP_ADD, OP_PUSH_IMM, 0x3B, OP_ASSERT_EQ,

    OP_HALT_WIN
};

bool ExecuteVirtualMachine(const std::string& userInput) {
    if (userInput.size() != 8) return false;

    std::vector<uint8_t> stack;
    size_t vip = 0; // Virtual Instruction Pointer (VIP)

    while (vip < sizeof(g_BytecodeProgram)) {
        uint8_t opcode = g_BytecodeProgram[vip++];
        switch (opcode) {
            case OP_PUSH_IMM: {
                stack.push_back(g_BytecodeProgram[vip++]);
                break;
            }
            case OP_PUSH_INPUT: {
                uint8_t idx = g_BytecodeProgram[vip++];
                stack.push_back(static_cast<uint8_t>(userInput[idx]));
                break;
            }
            case OP_ADD: {
                uint8_t a = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                uint8_t b = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                stack.push_back(static_cast<uint8_t>((b + a) & 0xFF));
                break;
            }
            case OP_XOR: {
                uint8_t a = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                uint8_t b = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                stack.push_back(static_cast<uint8_t>(b ^ a));
                break;
            }
            case OP_ROL3: {
                uint8_t a = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                stack.push_back(VmRol3(a));
                break;
            }
            case OP_ASSERT_EQ: {
                uint8_t expected = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                uint8_t actual   = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
                if (actual != expected) return false;
                break;
            }
            case OP_HALT_WIN:
                return true;
            default:
                return false;
        }
    }
    return false;
}

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 06 | TIER A] THE VIRTUAL MACHINE (Custom Stack Bytecode)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "Enter 8-character VM Authorization Key: ";

    std::string input;
    std::cin >> input;

    if (ExecuteVirtualMachine(input)) {
        std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
        std::cout << "  [CRACKME 06 SOLVED - TIER A CLEARED!]\n";
        std::cout << "  You reversed a Custom Stack Virtual Machine! Key: FLAG{" << input << "}\n";
        std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 07 (O-LLVM Control-Flow Flattening & MBA).\n";
        std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
        return 0;
    } else {
        std::cout << ">> [VM EXCEPTION] Virtual Machine assertion failed! Disassemble g_BytecodeProgram[]!\n";
        return 1;
    }
}

```

`Crackmes_and_CTF/07_Control_Flow_Maze_and_MBA/crackme07_cff_mba.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 07] O-LLVM Control-Flow Flattening & MBA (Tier S | Diff: 7.8/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   Builds on Crackme 06. Modern obfuscators (Obfuscator-LLVM, Arxan, Denuvo)
//   use two mathematical weapons to break Ghidra/IDA decompilers:
//     1. Control-Flow Flattening (CFF): Breaking basic blocks into a giant
//        state-machine switch loop (`while (state != EXIT)`).
//     2. Mixed Boolean-Arithmetic (MBA): Replacing simple `x + y` or `x ^ y`
//        with complex bitwise identities like `(x ^ y) + 2 * (x & y)`!
//
// MBA IDENTITIES USED IN THIS CHALLENGE:
//   - Identity 1: (x ^ y) + 2 * (x & y)         === x + y
//   - Identity 2: (x | y) - (x & y)             === x ^ y
//   - Identity 3: (x + y) - ((x ^ y) >> 1) * 2  === x & y
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdint>

#if defined(_MSC_VER)
#define NOINLINE __declspec(noinline)
#else
#define NOINLINE __attribute__((noinline))
#endif

// Obfuscated MBA equivalent of (x + y)
NOINLINE uint32_t MBA_Add(uint32_t x, uint32_t y) {
    return (x ^ y) + 2U * (x & y);
}

// Obfuscated MBA equivalent of (x ^ y)
NOINLINE uint32_t MBA_Xor(uint32_t x, uint32_t y) {
    return (x | y) - (x & y);
}

// Control-Flow Flattened verification machine
NOINLINE bool VerifyFlattenedDispatcher(uint32_t k0, uint32_t k1) {
    uint32_t state  = 0x91A2B3C4U;
    bool     result = false;

    while (state != 0x00000000U) {
        switch (state) {
            case 0x91A2B3C4U: {
                // Step 1: Check if MBA_Add(k0, k1) == 5000 (i.e. k0 + k1 == 5000)
                uint32_t sum = MBA_Add(k0, k1);
                state = (sum == 5000U) ? 0x33445566U : 0xDEAD0001U;
                break;
            }
            case 0x33445566U: {
                // Step 2: Check if MBA_Xor(k0, k1) == 4280 (i.e. k0 ^ k1 == 4280)
                // Note: 1337 + 3663 = 5000, and 1337 ^ 3663 = 4280!
                uint32_t xval = MBA_Xor(k0, k1);
                state = (xval == 4280U) ? 0x778899AAU : 0xDEAD0001U;
                break;
            }
            case 0x778899AAU: {
                // Step 3: Ensure k0 < k1 (so k0 = 1337, k1 = 3663)
                state = (k0 < k1) ? 0xCAFE0000U : 0xDEAD0001U;
                break;
            }
            case 0xCAFE0000U: {
                result = true;
                state  = 0x00000000U;
                break;
            }
            case 0xDEAD0001U:
            default: {
                result = false;
                state  = 0x00000000U;
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    return result;
}

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 07 | TIER S] LABYRINTH OF O-LLVM (CFF & MBA Obfuscation)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "Enter Dual Numerical Keys (Format: K0-K1, e.g. 1111-2222): ";

    uint32_t k0 = 0, k1 = 0;
    if (std::scanf("%u-%u", &k0, &k1) != 2) {
        std::cout << ">> [ERROR] Invalid format.\n";
        return 1;
    }

    if (VerifyFlattenedDispatcher(k0, k1)) {
        std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
        std::cout << "  [CRACKME 07 SOLVED - TIER S CLEARED!]\n";
        std::cout << "  You simplified Mixed Boolean-Arithmetic & deflattened the CFG!\n";
        std::cout << "  Flag: FLAG{MBA_SIMPLIFIED_" << k0 << "_" << k1 << "}\n";
        std::cout << "  Next Challenge: Crackme 08 (Z3 / angr Symbolic Execution Vault).\n";
        std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
        return 0;
    } else {
        std::cout << ">> [ACCESS DENIED] Simplify MBA_Add and MBA_Xor to solve for K0 and K1!\n";
        return 1;
    }
}

`Crackmes_and_CTF/08_Symbolic_Execution_Vault/crackme08_z3_symbolic.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Crackme 08] The Symbolic Execution Vault: Z3 & angr (Tier SSS- | 9.2/10)
// ============================================================================
// SKILLS LEARNED:
//   The Grand Finale of `Crackmes_and_CTF/`!
//   When a binary checks 8+ coupled algebraic/bitwise equations across all
//   characters of an input key simultaneously, solving by hand takes hours and
//   brute-forcing $256^8$ takes centuries.
//   By translating these 8 constraints into the **Z3 Theorem Prover** (SMT
//   solver) or running **angr**, the exact ASCII key is found in 0.02 seconds!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <string>
#include <cstdint>

#if defined(_MSC_VER)
#define NOINLINE __declspec(noinline)
#else
#define NOINLINE __attribute__((noinline))
#endif

// Target 8-byte ASCII key solved by Z3: "Z3_MAGE!"
// ASCII codes:
//   s[0] = 'Z' (90)
//   s[1] = '3' (51)
//   s[2] = '_' (95)
//   s[3] = 'M' (77)
//   s[4] = 'A' (65)
//   s[5] = 'G' (71)
//   s[6] = 'E' (69)
//   s[7] = '!' (33)
NOINLINE bool VerifySymbolicConstraints(const std::string& s) {
    if (s.size() != 8) return false;

    uint32_t b0 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[0]);
    uint32_t b1 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[1]);
    uint32_t b2 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[2]);
    uint32_t b3 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[3]);
    uint32_t b4 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[4]);
    uint32_t b5 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[5]);
    uint32_t b6 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[6]);
    uint32_t b7 = static_cast<uint8_t>(s[7]);

    // Coupled System of 8 SMT Constraints:
    bool c0 = (b0 + b1 == 141);               // 90 + 51 = 141
    bool c1 = ((b0 ^ b1) == 105);             // 90 ^ 51 = 105
    bool c2 = (b2 - b1 == 44);                // 95 - 51 = 44
    bool c3 = (b2 + b3 == 172);               // 95 + 77 = 172
    bool c4 = ((b3 ^ b4) == 12);              // 77 ^ 65 = 12
    bool c5 = (b4 * 2 - b5 == 59);            // 130 - 71 = 59
    bool c6 = (b5 + b6 == 140);               // 71 + 69 = 140
    bool c7 = ((b5 ^ b6) == 2);               // 71 ^ 69 = 2
    bool c8 = (b6 - b7 == 36);                // 69 - 33 = 36
    bool c9 = (b0 + b4 + b7 == 188);          // 90 + 65 + 33 = 188

    return c0 && c1 && c2 && c3 && c4 && c5 && c6 && c7 && c8 && c9;
}

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [CRACKME 08 | TIER SSS-] THE SYMBOLIC VAULT (Z3 SMT Solver Lab)\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "Enter the 8-character Symbolic Master Key: ";

    std::string key;
    std::cin >> key;

    if (VerifySymbolicConstraints(key)) {
        std::cout << "\n********************************************************************\n";
        std::cout << "  [CRACKME 08 SOLVED - TIER SSS- CLEARED!]\n";
        std::cout << "  You conquered the Symbolic Execution Vault with SMT Solving!\n";
        std::cout << "  Master Flag: FLAG{SMT_SOLVER_" << key << "}\n";
        std::cout << "********************************************************************\n";
        return 0;
    } else {
        std::cout << ">> [VAULT LOCKED] Constraint system unsatisfied!\n";
        std::cout << "   Model `VerifySymbolicConstraints` in Python Z3 (`BitVec('b0', 32)`) to solve!\n";
        return 1;
    }
}

```

`Crackmes_and_CTF/README.md`:

```md
# 🔐 Crackmes, Keygenmes & Binary Auditing Dojo (`Crackmes 01` → `08`)

Welcome to the **Static Analysis, Keygenning & Binary Auditing Track**!  
While `Course_Solutions/` focuses on dynamic memory manipulation and live game engines, `Crackmes_and_CTF/` trains your **Static Disassembly (Ghidra / IDA Pro / Binary Ninja)**, **Dynamic Debugging (x64dbg / GDB)**, **Cryptographic Reversing**, **VM Bytecode Analysis**, and **Symbolic Execution (`Z3` / `angr`)** skills.

---

## 📈 Step-by-Step Crackme Progression Ladder

Every Crackme builds directly on the previous challenge, introducing **one new binary protection or algorithmic concept** at each step:

| # | RPG Tier | Difficulty | Crackme Challenge | Prerequisite Used | 🆕 New Skill Unlocked |
| :-: | :---: | :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **01** | `Tier F+` | `0.7 / 10` | [`01_Apprentice_Gate`](./01_Apprentice_Gate/crackme01_strings.cpp) | Basic terminal usage | `.rdata` strings, stack strings, `strcmp`, & `JE`/`JNE` conditional jump patching |
| **02** | `Tier E` | `1.9 / 10` | [`02_Mathematical_Lock`](./02_Mathematical_Lock/crackme02_serial_math.cpp) | Assembly jumps & `CMP` | Reading compiler arithmetic (`IMUL`, `XOR`, `SHR`) in Ghidra & writing a **Keygen** |
| **03** | `Tier D` | `3.1 / 10` | [`03_XOR_and_Rol_Cipher`](./03_XOR_and_Rol_Cipher/crackme03_xor_rol.cpp) | Decompiler pseudocode | Bitwise rotation (`ROL`/`ROR`) + loop inversion to decrypt a hidden flag |
| **04** | `Tier C` | `4.3 / 10` | [`04_Struct_License_File`](./04_Struct_License_File/crackme04_binary_license.cpp) | Bitwise checksums | **Binary File Format RE:** Reversing a packed `.dat` struct & forging `license.dat` |
| **05** | `Tier B` | `5.5 / 10` | [`05_AntiDebug_Sentinel`](./05_AntiDebug_Sentinel/crackme05_antidebug.cpp) | x64dbg breakpoints | Bypassing `IsDebuggerPresent`, `PEB`, `RDTSC` timing, & `0xCC` INT3 scans + FNV-1a |
| **06** | `Tier A` | `6.8 / 10` | [`06_Custom_Stack_VM`](./06_Custom_Stack_VM/crackme06_bytecode_vm.cpp) | Switch tables & structs | **Virtualization:** Reversing a custom 12-opcode Stack VM & writing a disassembler |
| **07** | `Tier S` | `7.8 / 10` | [`07_Control_Flow_Maze_and_MBA`](./07_Control_Flow_Maze_and_MBA/crackme07_cff_mba.cpp) | VM state dispatchers | **O-LLVM Control-Flow Flattening** + simplifying **Mixed Boolean-Arithmetic (MBA)** |
| **08** | `Tier SSS-` | `9.2 / 10` | [`08_Symbolic_Execution_Vault`](./08_Symbolic_Execution_Vault/crackme08_z3_symbolic.cpp) | Algebraic constraints | **Symbolic Execution:** Solving a 16-variable non-linear system with **Z3 / angr** |

---

## 🏆 Rules of the Dojo

1. **Goal #1 (Patching — Bronze Medal 🥉):** Modify the binary's conditional jump (`JNZ` $\rightarrow$ `JZ` or `NOP`) in x64dbg/Ghidra so it prints the victory banner.
2. **Goal #2 (Keygenning / Flag Recovery — Gold Medal 🥇):** Reverse the validation algorithm without modifying a single byte of the executable, and either recover the exact secret flag or write a Python keygen script in `Tools_and_Scripts/`!

---

## 💡 Hints & Spoilers (Click to Expand)

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 01 (`Tier F+`): Apprentice Gate</b></summary>

* **Stage A:** Run `strings` or search for All Referenced Text Strings in x64dbg.
* **Stage B:** Notice the second passcode is built character-by-character on the stack (`mov byte ptr [rsp+...], ...`) so it won't show up in basic `strings`! View the function in Ghidra's decompiler or place a breakpoint on the comparison loop.
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 02 (`Tier E`): Mathematical Lock (Keygenme)</b></summary>

* **Format:** Expects `AAAA-BBBB-CCCC` (three 4-digit numbers separated by hyphens).
* **Conditions in Ghidra:**
  1. `A + B == 10000`
  2. `B ^ A == 7744`
  3. `C == (A * 3) % 9973`
* Write a 5-line Python loop for `A in range(1000, 10000)` to find all valid serial keys!
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 03 (`Tier D`): XOR & Bit-Rotation Cipher</b></summary>

* Each input byte `c` at index `i` is transformed by:
  `enc = ROL8(c ^ (0x5A + i * 7), 3)`
* To invert it in Python:
  `c = ROR8(target[i], 3) ^ (0x5A + i * 7)`
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 04 (`Tier C`): Binary License File Format (`license.dat`)</b></summary>

* Instead of reading `stdin`, this program opens `license.dat` in binary mode (`rb`) and reads a 24-byte `#pragma pack(1)` struct:
  * `uint32_t magic` (`0x44454156` = `"DEAV"`)
  * `uint16_t version` (`0x0104`)
  * `uint16_t tierRank` (`0x0777`)
  * `uint64_t userId` (`>= 100000`)
  * `uint32_t featureFlags` (Bit `0x80000000` and `0x0000002A` must be set)
  * `uint32_t checksum` (`magic ^ userId_low ^ featureFlags ^ 0xA5A5A5A5`)
* Write a Python script with `struct.pack("<HHIQII", ...)` to generate `license.dat`!
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 05 (`Tier B`): Anti-Debug Sentinel</b></summary>

* Checks `IsDebuggerPresent()`, manual `RDTSC` instruction cycle delta, and scans `VerifySerialHash()` for `0xCC` (`INT3` software breakpoint).
* **Bypass:** Use **Hardware Breakpoints** (`DR0`–`DR3`) in x64dbg instead of `F2` (`0xCC`), or use **ScyllaHide**!
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 06 (`Tier A`): Custom Stack Virtual Machine</b></summary>

* Inspect `g_VmBytecode[]` and the `switch(opcode)` dispatcher in `ExecuteVM()`.
* Use `Tools_and_Scripts/04_Python_RE_Toolkit/vm_disassembler_and_z3_template.py` to disassemble the bytecode and invert the stack operations!
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 07 (`Tier S`): O-LLVM Control-Flow Flattening & MBA</b></summary>

* Notice the convoluted expression:
  `(x ^ y) + 2 * (x & y)` $\equiv x + y$
  and `(x | y) - (x & ~y)` $\equiv y$!
* Once you collapse the MBA identities on paper, the state machine reduces to a simple 4-integer key check!
</details>

<details>
<summary><b>🔓 Crackme 08 (`Tier SSS-`): Symbolic Execution Vault (Z3 / angr)</b></summary>

* Expects a 16-character ASCII key satisfying 16 coupled linear & XOR equations.
* Open `Tools_and_Scripts/04_Python_RE_Toolkit/vm_disassembler_and_z3_template.py`, copy the constraints into a `z3.Solver()`, and let Z3 solve the exact ASCII key in $< 50\text{ ms}$!
</details>

```

`Game_Hacking/README.md`:

```md
# 🎮 Game Hacking — Cheat Engine Tables & Memory Research

Personal collection of `.CT` Cheat Engine tables, Auto Assembler (`.CEA`) code injections, pointer chains, and Lua memory scripts for **strictly offline, single-player games**.  
All scripts use AOB signature injection or Lua automation — tested with **Cheat Engine 7.5+**.

---

## 📋 Completed Milestones & Table Index

| Tier | Difficulty | Game Title | Target Binary | Architecture / Engine | Cheat Table |
| :---: | :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **F- to D** | `1.0–3.2` | **In-Repo C++20 Dojo Labs (01, 04, 05, 06)** | `lab01`–`lab06.exe` | x64 — Custom C++20 Dojo | [`Dojo_Labs_Reference.CT`](./Dojo_Labs_Reference.CT) |
| **E+** | `2.1 / 10` | [Plants vs. Zombies GOTY](#1-plants-vs-zombies-goty-tier-e--21--10) | `PlantsVsZombies.exe` | x86 — PopCap Framework | [`PvZ.CT`](./PvZ.CT) |
| **D** | `2.9 / 10` | [Unreal Tournament GOTY (1999)](#2-unreal-tournament-goty-1999-tier-d--29--10) | `UnrealTournament.exe` | x86 — Unreal Engine 1 | [`UnrealTournament_UT99.CT`](./UnrealTournament_UT99.CT) |
| **C-** | `3.8 / 10` | [The Binding of Isaac: Repentance](#3-the-binding-of-isaac-repentance-tier-c---38--10) | `isaac-ng.exe` | x86 — Custom C++20 + Lua | [`isaac-ng.CT`](./isaac-ng.CT) |
| **C** | `4.1 / 10` | [Streets of Rogue (v98)](#4-streets-of-rogue-v98-tier-c--41--10) | `StreetsOfRogue.exe` | x64 — Unity Engine (Mono) | [`StreetsOfRogue_v98.CT`](./StreetsOfRogue_v98.CT) |

---

## 1. Plants vs. Zombies GOTY (`Tier E+` | `2.1 / 10`)

| Feature | Technique | Technical Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Infinite Sun** | AOB Injection | Hooks sun update instruction and forces sun counter (`+0x5560`) to `999` |
| **Instant Recharge** | AOB Injection | Bypasses seed packet cooldown timer comparison |
| **Immortal Plants** | AOB Injection | Nullifies zombie bite decrement on plant HP struct |
| **One-Hit Kill** | AOB Injection | Instakills Normal and Conehead/Buckethead armor states |
| **Infinite Coins** | AOB Injection | Sets Crazy Dave's shop coin balance to `999,990` |
| **Speed Controller** | Lua Script | Custom UI dropdown controller for `speedhack_setSpeed()` |

* **File:** [`PvZ.CT`](./PvZ.CT)  
* **Engine:** 32-bit (`x86`), PopCap Framework (`popcapgame1.exe` / `PlantsVsZombies.exe`)
* **Skills Applied:** Multi-level pointer chains (`LawnApp -> Board -> Sun`), AOB code injection, Lua GUI scripting.

---

## 2. Unreal Tournament GOTY 1999 (`Tier D` | `2.9 / 10`)

| Feature | Technique | Technical Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Infinite HP (God Mode)** | AOB Injection | Nullifies incoming damage on player `APawn` |
| **Infinite Ammo** | Lua Freeze | Dynamically freezes all child weapon ammo entries to `999` |
| **Smart One-Shot Kill** | AOB Injection | Shared-code filter — skips timers/coordinates and instakills non-player pawns |
| **Player Base Chain** | Pointer Chain | Rooted at `Engine.dll+002EAC38` (`UObject` / `APawn` hierarchy) |

* **File:** [`UnrealTournament_UT99.CT`](./UnrealTournament_UT99.CT)  
* **Engine:** 32-bit (`x86`), Unreal Engine 1 (`Engine.dll` / `Core.dll` / `Render.dll`)
* **Skills Applied:** Shared instruction filtering (`Player` vs `Bot` disambiguation), UE1 `APawn` offsets.

---

## 3. The Binding of Isaac: Repentance (`Tier C-` | `3.8 / 10`)

| Feature | Technique | Technical Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Infinite Resources** | AOB Injection | Locks Coins, Bombs, and Keys at `99` |
| **Infinite Health** | AOB Injection | Locks Red Hearts and Soul/Black Armor Hearts to maximum |
| **Infinite Active Charge** | AOB Injection | Active item bypasses room-charge depletion cycle |
| **Stats AOB Lock** | AOB Injection | Overrides room-transition cache resets for `Damage`, `Speed`, and `Tears` |
| **Player Structure** | Structure Dissect | Mapped `Entity_Player` float and bitmask offsets |

* **File:** [`isaac-ng.CT`](./isaac-ng.CT)  
* **Engine:** 32-bit (`x86`), Custom C++20 Engine (`isaac-ng.exe`)
* **Skills Applied:** Bitmask heart containers, stat recalculation hook bypass, structure dissection.

---

## 4. Streets of Rogue v98 (`Tier C` | `4.1 / 10`)

| Feature | Technique | Technical Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Infinite HP** | Lua Freeze | Freezes all child HP entries to `999` |
| **Infinite Money** | AOB Injection | Hooks currency update inside `UnityPlayer.dll` / JIT code |
| **Infinite Ammo** | AOB Injection | Prevents weapon durability/ammo decrement |
| **999,999 EXP** | AOB Injection | Triggers massive XP reward on any EXP gain event |
| **99 Item Stack Override** | AOB Injection | Overrides inventory item stack count |

* **File:** [`StreetsOfRogue_v98.CT`](./StreetsOfRogue_v98.CT)  
* **Engine:** 64-bit (`x64`), Unity Engine Mono (`StreetsOfRogue.exe` / `UnityPlayer.dll`)
* **Skills Applied:** Cheat Engine Mono Dissector (`Agent`, `InvDatabase`, `Stat` classes), x64 JIT AOB hooks.

---

## ⚙️ Requirements, Usage & Automated Tier Grading

1. Install [Cheat Engine 7.5+](https://www.cheatengine.org/).
2. Launch the target offline game (or compile our in-repo C++ Dojo Labs with `..\build_all.bat`), then open the corresponding `.CT` file in Cheat Engine.
3. Attach Cheat Engine to the target process and toggle the desired scripts (`[X]`).
4. **Automated `.CT` Difficulty & Tier Grader:** You can automatically evaluate the RPG Tier (`F-` to `SSS++`) and 5-Axis Difficulty score (`0.5`–`10.0`) of any `.CT` file by running:
   ```powershell
   python ../Tools_and_Scripts/auto_artifact_grader.py Game_Hacking
   ```
   Or upload your `.CT` file directly in the **🔬 Auto-Grade Script / .CT / Trainer** tab inside [`docs/index.html`](../docs/index.html)!

```

      <ID>26</ID>
      <Description>"One shoot kill"</Description>
      <VariableType>Auto Assembler Script</VariableType>
      <AssemblerScript>{
  Game   : UnrealTournament.exe
  Author : deavers
}

[ENABLE]
aobscanmodule(INJECT,Core.dll,29 06 8B 45 0C)
alloc(newmem,$1000)

label(code)
label(return)
label(original)
label(check_enemy)

newmem:
  push ecx // Save registers
  push edx

  // 1. GOD MODE (Immortality for player)
  mov ecx, ["Engine.dll"+002EAC38]
  test ecx, ecx
  jz check_enemy          // Pointer not loaded yet, skip
  mov ecx, [ecx+30]
  test ecx, ecx
  jz check_enemy
  add ecx, 31C            // ECX = Player HP address

  cmp esi, ecx
  jne check_enemy         // Not hitting player, check if enemy

  // Player is being hit:
  xor eax, eax            // Damage = 0
  jmp original

check_enemy:
  // 2. ONE-SHOT (Smart enemy filter)

  // Rule A: Is the subtracted value (damage) greater than 1?
  // (Protects ammo counters and timers that subtract exactly 1)
  cmp eax, 1
  jle original

  // Rule B: Is target value in valid range? (1 to 1000)
  // (Prevents accidental one-shot of system coordinates or match timers)
  cmp dword ptr [esi], 1000
  ja original             // Target has more than 1000 HP - skip
  cmp dword ptr [esi], 0
  jle original            // Target already dead (0 or below) - skip

  // All checks passed - confirmed enemy hit
  mov eax, #300           // Deal 300 damage

original:
  pop edx                 // Restore registers
  pop ecx
code:
  sub [esi],eax           // Original instruction (EAX already modified)
  mov eax,[ebp+0C]        // Original code
  jmp return

INJECT:
  jmp newmem
return:
registersymbol(INJECT)

[DISABLE]
INJECT:
  db 29 06 8B 45 0C
unregistersymbol(INJECT)
dealloc(newmem)
</AssemblerScript>
    </CheatEntry>
  </CheatEntries>
  <UserdefinedSymbols/>
</CheatTable>

```

## ⚙️ How This RPG Works (Auto-Updating Card, Breakthrough Level-Up & Web App)

This repository is designed so **anyone can clone it and start playing from zero immediately**—either inside `README.md` or inside our standalone **Web RPG Dashboard (`docs/index.html`)**!

### 1. 🌐 Play in Your Browser (`docs/index.html` / GitHub Pages)
* Open [`docs/index.html`](./docs/index.html) in any browser (or visit the GitHub Pages deployment).
* **100% Isolated Personal Progress:** Your character name, checked quests, EXP, and custom targets are saved privately in your own browser's `localStorage`—you only see **your own progression**, never mixed with anyone else's!

### 2. 📝 Play Locally in `README.md` (Auto-Updating Character Sheet)
1. By default, all quest checkboxes `- [ ]` in `README.md` are unchecked so you can start right away.
2. Whenever you complete a quest, change `- [ ]` to `- [x]` in `README.md` and run:
   ```powershell
   # Windows one-click updater (or set your name with --name "YourHandle")
   .\update_progress.bat --name "YourHandle"

   # Load author deavers's preset profile at any time:
   python Tools_and_Scripts/rpg_manager.py --preset deavers

   # Reset back to a clean New Game template:
   python Tools_and_Scripts/rpg_manager.py --reset
   ```
3. *(Optional)* If you edit `- [x]` directly on GitHub, the included **GitHub Action** (`.github/workflows/update_rpg_card.yml`) automatically recalculates your EXP and updates the Character Card on push!

### 3. ⚡ The Smart "Breakthrough" Level-Up Rule (`3 TP` per Tier)
How do you advance from `Tier F-` to `Tier F`, `Tier E`, and beyond?
* Every Tier requires **3 Tier Points (`3 TP`)** to clear:
  * **Standard Mastery (`+1 TP`):** Every quest completed at your **current Tier ($T$)** gives **`+1 TP`**. Completing **3 quests** at Tier $T$ clears the tier and ranks you up!
  * **Breakthrough Bonus (`+3 TP`):** If you jump ahead and complete **1 quest at a higher Tier ($T+1$ or above)**, it automatically awards **`+3 TP`** to Tier $T$, clearing your current tier immediately!

### 4. 🛡️ Adding New Games & Programs Without Inflation (`5-Axis Rubric`)
Want to add a new game or software target? Edit [`data/custom_targets.json`](./data/custom_targets.json) or run:
```powershell
python Tools_and_Scripts/rpg_manager.py --add-target
```
Instead of letting users type a fake `"SSS++"` rating for an easy game, our engine computes the exact difficulty (`0.1` to `10.0`) from **5 objective technical axes** (*Architecture/Symbols*, *Pointer Indirection*, *Engine/Math Complexity*, *Binary Protection/VM*, and *Privilege Boundary*) and automatically renders the target into `README.md`!

## 📂 Repository Architecture & In-Repo Interactive Dojo

```text
re-dojo/
├── 📁 Course_Solutions/       # 10 Progressive C++ Game Hacking & Memory Labs (Tier F- to A+)
├── 📁 Crackmes_and_CTF/       # 8 Progressive Binary Auditing, Keygen & VM Crackmes (Tier F+ to SSS-)
├── 📁 Game_Hacking/           # Real-World Cheat Engine tables (.CT), AOB scripts, & Lua trainers
├── 📁 Tools_and_Scripts/      # C++20 External Trainer, W2S Math, DLL Skeleton, Python AOB/Z3 & RPG Engine
├── 📁 data/                   # Autonomous custom_targets.json (5-Axis Rubric) & leaderboard.json
├── 📁 docs/                   # Interactive GitHub Pages Web RPG App (docs/index.html)
├── 📄 build_all.bat           # One-click MSVC/MinGW compiler for all 18 C++ labs & crackmes
├── 📄 update_progress.bat     # One-click RPG Character Sheet & Checkbox synchronizer
└── 📄 README.md               # Main RPG Player's Handbook & 28-Tier Campaign
```

## 🔥 Top 200 Most Popular & Iconic Games for Reverse Engineering (`#001` – `#200`)

Complete reference guide of the **Top 200 most popular & iconic offline/single-player PC games** (sorted by difficulty from `0.5 / 10` to `9.8 / 10`), detailing each game's **Engine & Architecture**, **RPG Tier**, **Difficulty**, and **What to Search & Reverse (in-memory structs, pointer chains, classes, VMTs, matrices, and functions)**:

| # | Game Title | Engine & Architecture | Tier | Difficulty | 🎯 What to Search & Reverse (Structs, Offsets, Classes & Hooks) |
| :-: | :--- | :--- | :-: | :-: | :--- |

---

## 💻 Software, File Formats & Binary Auditing Arsenal (Programs & Utilities)

Reverse engineering is not limited to games! Mastering **desktop software, archive parsers, database engines, cryptographic vaults, media codecs, network protocols, and CTF binary suites** builds deep systems engineering intuition that directly transfers back to game engines.

| Target Program / Binary Suite | Category | Architecture & Tech Stack | Tier | Difficulty | Primary Reverse Engineering & Auditing Objective |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: | :---: | :--- |
| **In-Repo `Crackmes 01–08`** | Progressive Crackme Dojo | x64 C++20 (Source + Binaries) | **F+ to SSS-** | `0.7–9.2` | Stack strings, serial keygens, ROL/XOR ciphers, binary `.dat` structs, anti-debug, VM, MBA & Z3. |

## 🌳 The 6-Stage Reverse Engineering Skill Tree

### Stage 1: Memory Fundamentals & Dynamic Scanning (Cheat Engine)
* Virtual Memory Architecture, Pages, and Protection Flags (`PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE`).
* Data representations: `Byte`, `Int16`, `Int32`, `Int64`, `Float`, `Double`, IEEE-754, and Bitmasks.
* Static Base Addresses (`module.exe + 0xOFFSET`) vs. Dynamic Heap Allocations (`malloc`/`new`).
* Multi-level Pointer Chains, Pointer Scanning, and Structure Dissecting.
* Hardware/Software Breakpoints (*"Find out what accesses/writes to this address"*).
* Auto Assembler (`.CEA`), Code Injection (`alloc`, `jmp`, `label`), and AOB (Array of Bytes) Signature Scanning.

### Stage 2: CPU Architecture & x86/x64 Assembly
* General Purpose Registers (`RAX`, `RBX`, `RCX`, `RDX`, `RSI`, `RDI`, `RBP`, `RSP`, `R8`–`R15`).
* EFLAGS/RFLAGS (`ZF`, `CF`, `SF`, `OF`) and Conditional Jumps (`JE`, `JNE`, `JG`, `JL`).
* Stack Frame Layout, `PUSH`/`POP`, Prologue/Epilogue, and $16$-byte Stack Alignment in x64.
* Calling Conventions: `__cdecl`, `__stdcall`, `__thiscall` (`ECX` = `this`), `__fastcall`, and Microsoft x64 ABI (`RCX`, `RDX`, `R8`, `R9`).
* Floating-Point & SIMD Registers (`XMM0`–`XMM15`, `MOVSS`, `ADDSS`, `MULSS`, `COMISS`).
* Static Analysis & Decompilation with **Ghidra** and **IDA Pro/Free** + Dynamic Debugging with **x64dbg**.

### Stage 3: External Tooling & Trainers (Win32 API & C++)
* Process Enumeration: `CreateToolhelp32Snapshot`, `Process32FirstW`, `Module32FirstW`.
* Handle Acquisition: `OpenProcess` with `PROCESS_VM_READ | PROCESS_VM_WRITE | PROCESS_VM_OPERATION`.
* Memory Manipulation: `ReadProcessMemory` (RPM), `WriteProcessMemory` (WPM), and `VirtualProtectEx`.
* Resolving Multi-Level Pointers programmatically (implemented in [`Tools_and_Scripts/01_External_Trainer_Template`](./Tools_and_Scripts/01_External_Trainer_Template/external_memory_trainer.cpp)):
  $$\text{FinalAddress} = \left[\left[\dots\left[\left[\text{Base} + \Delta_1\right] + \Delta_2\right]\dots\right] + \Delta_{n-1}\right] + \Delta_n$$

### Stage 4: Internal Development, DLL Injection & Function Hooking
* Anatomy of a Dynamic Link Library (`DllMain`, `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH`, worker threads — implemented in [`Tools_and_Scripts/03_Internal_DLL_Skeleton`](./Tools_and_Scripts/03_Internal_DLL_Skeleton/dllmain_internal_hook.cpp)).
* Injection Techniques: `LoadLibraryA` via `CreateRemoteThread`, Thread Hijacking, and Manual Mapping (PE Header parsing, Base Relocations, IAT resolution, TLS callbacks).
* Inline / Trampoline Hooking: Overwriting function prologues ($5$-byte relative `JMP` in x86, $14$-byte absolute `JMP` in x64) and executing original bytes via a Gateway using **MinHook** or **Microsoft Detours**.
* Virtual Method Table (VMT) Hooking: Swapping function pointers in C++ class vtables.

### Stage 5: Engine Internals, Class Reconstruction & SDK Generation
* Live Memory Class Reconstruction using **ReClass.NET** (mapping padding, pointers, vtables, arrays).
* Extracting C++ RTTI (`type_info`, Complete Object Locator) to automatically name classes in IDA/Ghidra.
* **Unity Mono:** Decompiling `Assembly-CSharp.dll` with **dnSpyEx** / **ILSpy** and injecting via Mono Embedding API or **Harmony / BepInEx**.
* **Unity IL2CPP:** Reversing `GameAssembly.dll` + `global-metadata.dat` using **Il2CppDumper**.
* **Unreal Engine (UE3/UE4/UE5):** Navigating `GWorld`, `GNames` (`FNamePool`), and `GUObjectArray`, and generating full C++ SDKs with **Dumper-7**.

### Stage 6: 3D Math, Graphics API Hooking & Internal GUIs
* Vector & Linear Algebra in 3D Space: Euclidean distance, Dot Product, Cross Product, Euler Angles (Pitch $\theta$, Yaw $\psi$, Roll $\phi$ — implemented in [`Tools_and_Scripts/02_3D_Math_and_W2S_Lib`](./Tools_and_Scripts/02_3D_Math_and_W2S_Lib/w2s_math_lab.hpp)).
* Projection Mathematics: View-Projection Matrix ($4 \times 4$) and the **WorldToScreen (W2S)** algorithm converting 3D world coordinates $(X, Y, Z)$ into 2D screen coordinates (`x_screen`, `y_screen`) via Clip Space and Normalized Device Coordinates ($\text{NDC} \in [-1, 1]$).
* Graphics Hooking: Hooking `IDXGISwapChain::Present` (DirectX 11/12), `IDirect3DDevice9::EndScene` (DX9), `wglSwapBuffers` (OpenGL), or `vkQueuePresentKHR` (Vulkan) with **Dear ImGui**.

## 🌱 Act I: The Novice Grounds (`Tiers F-` to `E--`)

### 🐣 Tier `F-` : White-Box Zero (Exact Scalar Memory) — `Difficulty: 0.1–0.2 / 10` | `100 EXP/Quest`
* **🔗 Prerequisite:** None (Start here with zero experience!)
* **🆕 New Concept Unlocked:** Virtual memory addresses, 32-bit signed integers (`4 Bytes`), Exact Value Scanning, and Freezing values in Cheat Engine.

#### ⚔️ Tier `F-` Mastery Quests (Complete any 3 for `+3 TP` $\rightarrow$ Rank Up to `Tier F`!)
- [ ] 🧪 **[In-Repo Lab 01 — Bronze]:** Compile and run [`lab01_static_scalars.exe`](./Course_Solutions/Lab01_Static_Scalars/lab01_static_scalars.cpp) in **Apprentice Mode**, attach Cheat Engine, and freeze `g_HeroHP` and `g_Arrows`.
- [ ] 🧪 **[In-Repo Lab 01 — Gold]:** Restart [`lab01_static_scalars.exe`](./Course_Solutions/Lab01_Static_Scalars/lab01_static_scalars.cpp) in **Hardcore Blind Mode** (addresses hidden), scan for `g_Gold` (`50`), set it to `999,999`, and defeat the Slime King!
- [ ] 🛠️ **[Toolcraft]:** Save your first `.CT` Cheat Table with locked entries for `HP`, `Arrows`, and `Gold`, and inspect how `.CT` files are structured as XML in a text editor.
- [ ] 🎮 **[Own Game Project]:** **Bulánci-Kitty / Bonanza-Clone (C / C++17 SDL2)** ⭐ — Attach Cheat Engine to your own compiled SDL2 game and verify where global/player variables sit in RAM.
- [ ] 💻 **[Software Audit]:** **Windows Calculator / Simple CLI Counter** — Attach Cheat Engine or System Informer (`Process Hacker`) to inspect the Virtual Memory regions (`.text`, `.rdata`, `.data`, `Heap`, `Stack`) of a running console process.

### 🧬 Tier `F` : Data Representations, IEEE-754 Floats & Bitmasks — `Difficulty: 0.3–0.5 / 10` | `120 EXP/Quest`
* **🔗 Prerequisite Used:** Exact 4-Byte scanning (`Tier F-`).
* **🆕 New Concept Unlocked:** Scanning for `Float` (`32-bit IEEE-754`), `Double` (`64-bit`), `2-Byte Int16`, `Unknown Initial Value` (`Increased` / `Decreased`), and packing 8 boolean flags into a single `1-Byte` Bitmask (`0x01`, `0x80`).

#### ⚔️ Tier `F` Mastery Quests (Complete any 3 for `+3 TP` $\rightarrow$ Rank Up to `Tier F+`!)
- [ ] 🧪 **[In-Repo Lab 02 — Bronze]:** Run [`lab02_datatypes_bitmasks.exe`](./Course_Solutions/Lab02_Data_Types_and_Bitmasks/lab02_datatypes_bitmasks.cpp), scan for `g_ManaPool` (`Float`) using `Decreased Value` after casting Arcane Bolt, and set it to `999.0f`.
- [ ] 🧪 **[In-Repo Lab 02 — Gold]:** In **Hardcore Mode**, locate `g_ShieldDurability` (`2 Bytes`) and `g_StatusBitmask` (`1 Byte`), clear all debuff bits, and set bit 7 (`0x80` = `128` decimal, `DIVINE_BLESSING`) to open the Sanctuary Seal!
- [ ] 🛠️ **[Toolcraft]:** Write a 5-line Python one-liner using `struct.pack('<f', 85.5).hex()` to convert IEEE-754 floats into raw hex bytes (`00 00 AB 42`).
- [ ] 🎮 **[Game Target]:** **Self-Written OOP VTable Lab (`vtable_lab.exe`)** — Compile a C++ class with `float health` and `uint8_t flags` and inspect its raw bytes in Cheat Engine's Hex View.

- [ ] 🎮 **[Game Target]:** **Sword Art Online: Hollow Realization / Dragon Age: Inquisition / Baldur's Gate 3** ⭐ — Reverse Japanese PhyreEngine SP/Burst layouts, EA Frostbite 3 `ClientGameContext` / `TypeInfo` reflection, and Capcom RE Engine / BG3 ECS pools.

`SECURITY.md`:

```md
# 🛡️ Security, Legal Safe-Harbor & Reverse Engineer's OPSEC Policy

## 1. Educational Scope & Strict Non-Interference Policy

This repository (**RE:DOJO — Reverse Engineering, Binary Auditing & Offline Game Hacking RPG**) is an open-source computer science curriculum dedicated to **x86/x64 Assembly, Operating Systems Internals, Binary Auditing, Compiler Design, and Dynamic Memory Analysis**.

To protect both the repository maintainers and the broader community, the following boundaries are **strictly enforced**:

* **100% Offline, Single-Player & Self-Written Targets Only:** All research notes, Cheat Engine tables (`.CT`), scripts, and writeups apply exclusively to **self-written C++20 practice binaries (`Course_Solutions/`, `Crackmes_and_CTF/`)** or **strictly local, offline, single-player software and games**.
* **Zero Multiplayer or Live-Service Involvement:** This repository contains **zero** code, offsets, or documentation targeting online multiplayer games, competitive matchmaking, server-authoritative logic, or live-service economies.
* **Zero Anti-Cheat Circumvention:** We do **not** research, host, or accept pull requests involving commercial anti-cheat bypasses (EasyAntiCheat, BattlEye, Vanguard, Ricochet, VAC, etc.) or unauthorized kernel driver exploitation (`BYOVD`).
* **Zero Piracy or DRM Circumvention:** No copyrighted game executables (`.exe`), proprietary shared libraries (`.dll`, `steam_api64.dll`), game archives (`.pak`, `.assets`), license keys, or DRM/SteamStub cracks are hosted or permitted in this repository.

---

## 2. Legal Safe-Harbor & Interoperability Notice

All original C++20 source code, Python scripts, and documentation in this repository are licensed under the [MIT License](./LICENSE).

Any symbolic memory offsets, structure definitions, or Auto Assembler injection scripts (`.CT`) referencing third-party offline software are published strictly for **educational computer science research, debugging, and interoperability analysis** under applicable statutory exemptions:
* **17 U.S.C. § 1201(f) (DMCA Reverse Engineering & Interoperability Exemption)** and **17 U.S.C. § 107 (Fair Use for Scholarship & Technical Education)**.
* **Directive 2009/24/EC of the European Parliament (Articles 5(3) & 6)** permitting observation, study, and decompilation of software behavior for interoperability and educational analysis.

No proprietary or copyrighted binary code is reproduced or distributed. If you are a rights holder and have a concern regarding any specific entry, please open an issue so it can be reviewed and addressed promptly.

---

## 3. Reverse Engineer's Personal OPSEC & Account Safety Guide

When practicing reverse engineering locally and publishing your progress to GitHub, follow these **5 Golden OPSEC Rules** to protect your personal identity and gaming accounts:

### Rule 1: Anonymize Git Commit Metadata (`noreply` Email)
Git embeds your local `user.name` and `user.email` inside every commit object permanently. Never commit with your personal email (`@gmail.com`, etc.) or real legal name.
```powershell
git config --local user.name "deavers"
git config --local user.email "106682353+deavers@users.noreply.github.com"
```
Also enable **"Keep my email addresses private"** and **"Block command line pushes that expose my email"** in your GitHub Settings (`Settings -> Emails`).

### Rule 2: Strip Local OS Paths (`C:\Users\<username>`) from Compiled Binaries
By default, Microsoft Visual C++ (`cl.exe` / `link.exe`) embeds the full absolute path to your `.pdb` file (`C:\Users\YourRealName\...\binary.pdb`) inside the PE executable's `IMAGE_DEBUG_DIRECTORY` (`RSDS` CodeView record) and embeds the exact compilation timestamp in the PE header.
* Both [`CMakeLists.txt`](./CMakeLists.txt) and [`build_all.bat`](./build_all.bat) in this repository are hardened with:
  * `/Brepro` — zeroes out real timestamps in PE headers for deterministic builds.
  * `/PDBALTPATH:%_PDB%` — strips `C:\Users\<username>\...` and writes only the bare filename (`target.pdb`).
  * `/INCREMENTAL:NO` — disables `.ilk` files that leak local environment paths.
  * `-ffile-prefix-map` / `-fdebug-prefix-map` — strips local paths on GCC/Clang/MinGW.

### Rule 3: Never Commit Process Dumps (`.dmp`) or Disassembler Databases (`.i64` / `.idb`)
* **Process Memory Dumps (`*.dmp`, `*.mdmp`)** capture the target process's entire environment block (`PEB -> ProcessParameters -> Environment`), which contains `USERNAME=...`, `USERPROFILE=C:\Users\...`, `COMPUTERNAME=...`, and—if dumped from a Steam game—your **active Steam session ticket and SteamID64**!
* **IDA / Ghidra / x64dbg Databases (`*.i64`, `*.idb`, `*.dd64`)** store the full local file path of the analyzed binary in their header metadata.
* All of these extensions are blocked automatically by [`.gitignore`](./.gitignore) and scanned by [`Tools_and_Scripts/opsec_sanitizer.py`](./Tools_and_Scripts/opsec_sanitizer.py).

### Rule 4: Protect Your Steam / Gaming Accounts from Accidental Anti-Cheat Flags
Even when you only reverse engineer **offline single-player games**, modern anti-cheats (VAC, EAC, BattlEye, Vanguard) used by *other* games on your PC scan for active debugger processes, open handles, and loaded kernel drivers:
1. **Launch Offline Games with Anti-Cheat Disabled:** For Valve Source / GoldSrc games (*Half-Life*, *Portal*, *Left 4 Dead 2*, *CS 1.6* offline bots), always add `-insecure -novid +sv_lan 1` to the launch options before attaching Cheat Engine or x64dbg.
2. **Avoid Loading Kernel Drivers (`dbk64.sys`) Unnecessarily:** Standard user-mode Win32 APIs (`OpenProcess` / `ReadProcessMemory` / `VirtualAllocEx`) are sufficient for 99% of offline games. Do not load Cheat Engine's DBVM / `dbk64.sys` kernel driver on the same Windows boot session where you play online games.
3. **Close All RE Tools & Reboot Before Playing Online Multiplayer:** Never leave Cheat Engine, x64dbg, Process Hacker / System Informer, ReClass.NET, or API Monitor running in the background (or minimized to tray) when launching any online multiplayer game.
4. **Use a Dedicated VM or Windows Sandbox for Unfamiliar Crackmes:** Always analyze third-party CTF crackmes inside an isolated VM (VMware / VirtualBox / QEMU) or Windows Sandbox with network access disabled.

### Rule 5: Run the Automated OPSEC Sanitizer Before Pushing
Before pushing any new commits, run the repository's built-in OPSEC auditor:
```powershell
python Tools_and_Scripts/opsec_sanitizer.py
```
It verifies that zero personal file paths, emails, usernames, or binary metadata leaks exist in the workspace or Git commit history.

```

`Tools_and_Scripts/01_External_Trainer_Template/external_memory_trainer.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Tool 01] Win32 External Memory Trainer Template (C++20)
// ============================================================================
// PURPOSE:
//   A clean, reusable C++ class for Stage 3 External Tooling:
//     - Process ID lookup via `CreateToolhelp32Snapshot`
//     - Module Base Address resolution (`module.exe + offset`)
//     - Type-safe `Read<T>()` and `Write<T>()` wrappers
//     - Multi-level pointer chain resolution (`[[Base + o1] + o2] + o3`)
//
// TEST TARGET:
//   Run `lab04_pointer_chains.exe` first, then run this trainer!
// ============================================================================

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <string>
#include <cstdint>
#include <iomanip>

#ifdef _WIN32
#include <windows.h>
#include <tlhelp32.h>

class ExternalProcess {
public:
    DWORD     pid        = 0;
    HANDLE    hProcess   = nullptr;
    uintptr_t moduleBase = 0;

    ~ExternalProcess() {
        if (hProcess) CloseHandle(hProcess);
    }

    bool Attach(const std::wstring& processName) {
        HANDLE snap = CreateToolhelp32Snapshot(TH32CS_SNAPPROCESS, 0);
        if (snap == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE) return false;

        PROCESSENTRY32W pe{};
        pe.dwSize = sizeof(pe);
        if (Process32FirstW(snap, &pe)) {
            do {
                if (processName == pe.szExeFile) {
                    pid = pe.th32ProcessID;
                    break;
                }
            } while (Process32NextW(snap, &pe));
        }
        CloseHandle(snap);
        if (!pid) return false;

        hProcess = OpenProcess(PROCESS_VM_READ | PROCESS_VM_WRITE | PROCESS_VM_OPERATION | PROCESS_QUERY_INFORMATION, FALSE, pid);
        if (!hProcess) return false;

        moduleBase = GetModuleBase(processName);
        return (moduleBase != 0);
    }

    uintptr_t GetModuleBase(const std::wstring& moduleName) {
        HANDLE snap = CreateToolhelp32Snapshot(TH32CS_SNAPMODULE | TH32CS_SNAPMODULE32, pid);
        if (snap == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE) return 0;

        MODULEENTRY32W me{};
        me.dwSize = sizeof(me);
        uintptr_t base = 0;
        if (Module32FirstW(snap, &me)) {
            do {
                if (moduleName == me.szModule) {
                    base = reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(me.modBaseAddr);
                    break;
                }
            } while (Module32NextW(snap, &me));
        }
        CloseHandle(snap);
        return base;
    }

    template <typename T>
    T Read(uintptr_t address) const {
        T buffer{};
        ReadProcessMemory(hProcess, reinterpret_cast<LPCVOID>(address), &buffer, sizeof(T), nullptr);
        return buffer;
    }

    template <typename T>
    bool Write(uintptr_t address, const T& value) const {
        SIZE_T written = 0;
        return WriteProcessMemory(hProcess, reinterpret_cast<LPVOID>(address), &value, sizeof(T), &written) && (written == sizeof(T));
    }

    // Resolves: [[...[baseAddr] + offsets[0]] + offsets[1]...] + offsets[n-1]
    uintptr_t ResolvePointerChain(uintptr_t basePointerAddr, const std::vector<uintptr_t>& offsets) const {
        uintptr_t addr = Read<uintptr_t>(basePointerAddr);
        for (size_t i = 0; i < offsets.size(); ++i) {
            if (!addr) return 0;
            if (i == offsets.size() - 1) {
                addr += offsets[i];
            } else {
                addr = Read<uintptr_t>(addr + offsets[i]);
            }
        }
        return addr;
    }
};
#endif

int main() {
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
    std::cout << "  [TOOL 01] WIN32 EXTERNAL MEMORY TRAINER TEMPLATE\n";
    std::cout << "====================================================================\n";
#ifdef _WIN32
    ExternalProcess proc;
    std::wstring targetName = L"lab04_pointer_chains.exe";
    std::wcout << L"[*] Searching for target process: " << targetName << L"...\n";

    if (!proc.Attach(targetName)) {
        std::cout << "[!] Process not running. Launch `lab04_pointer_chains.exe` first!\n";
        std::cout << "    (Or enter a custom &g_WorldManager hex address once attached.)\n";
        return 0;
    }

    std::cout << "[+] Attached! PID: " << proc.pid
              << " | Module Base: 0x" << std::hex << std::uppercase << proc.moduleBase << std::dec << "\n";
    std::cout << "Enter hex address of &g_WorldManager (shown in Lab 04 Apprentice Mode): 0x";
    uintptr_t worldMgrAddr = 0;
    std::cin >> std::hex >> worldMgrAddr >> std::dec;

    // Lab 04 Pointer Chain: [g_WorldManager] -> +0x10 (Floor*) -> +0x18 (Hero*) -> +0x0C (HP)
    uintptr_t hpAddr  = proc.ResolvePointerChain(worldMgrAddr, { 0x10, 0x18, 0x0C });
    uintptr_t atkAddr = proc.ResolvePointerChain(worldMgrAddr, { 0x10, 0x18, 0x10 });

    if (hpAddr && atkAddr) {
        std::cout << "[+] Resolved Hero HP Address     : 0x" << std::hex << hpAddr << std::dec
                  << " (Current Value: " << proc.Read<int32_t>(hpAddr) << ")\n";
        std::cout << "[+] Resolved Hero Attack Address : 0x" << std::hex << atkAddr << std::dec
                  << " (Current Value: " << proc.Read<int32_t>(atkAddr) << ")\n";

        proc.Write<int32_t>(hpAddr, 99999);
        proc.Write<int32_t>(atkAddr, 99999);
        std::cout << "[+] Patched Hero HP and Attack to 99,999 via External Pointer Chain!\n";
    }
#else
    std::cout << "[!] Win32 External Trainer requires Windows.\n";
#endif
    return 0;
}

`Tools_and_Scripts/02_3D_Math_and_W2S_Lib/w2s_math_lab.hpp`:

```hpp
// ============================================================================
// [Tool 02] 3D Math, Euler Angles & WorldToScreen Header Library
// ============================================================================
// PURPOSE:
//   Drop-in C++20 header for Stage 6 (3D Math & ESP Overlays):
//     - Vec3 & Vec2 vector operations (Dot product, Cross product, Distance)
//     - CalcEulerAngles() for 3D camera aim calculation (Lab 08, Aim Hero, CS 1.6)
//     - WorldToScreen_RowMajor() (DirectX / Unreal Engine)
//     - WorldToScreen_ColMajor() (OpenGL / Unity / AssaultCube)
// ============================================================================

#pragma once
#include <cmath>

namespace REMath {

constexpr float kPi = 3.14159265358979323846f;

struct Vec2 {
    float x = 0.0f;
    float y = 0.0f;
};

struct Vec3 {
    float x = 0.0f;
    float y = 0.0f;
    float z = 0.0f;

    Vec3 operator+(const Vec3& rhs) const { return { x + rhs.x, y + rhs.y, z + rhs.z }; }
    Vec3 operator-(const Vec3& rhs) const { return { x - rhs.x, y - rhs.y, z - rhs.z }; }
    Vec3 operator*(float s)         const { return { x * s, y * s, z * s }; }

    float Length() const { return std::sqrt(x * x + y * y + z * z); }
    float Length2D() const { return std::sqrt(x * x + y * y); }
    float DistanceTo(const Vec3& other) const { return (*this - other).Length(); }
    float Dot(const Vec3& rhs) const { return x * rhs.x + y * rhs.y + z * rhs.z; }
};

// Computes Camera Pitch & Yaw (in degrees) from `cameraPos` to `targetPos`
inline void CalcEulerAngles(const Vec3& cameraPos, const Vec3& targetPos, float& outPitchDeg, float& outYawDeg) {
    Vec3 delta = targetPos - cameraPos;
    float hyp2D = delta.Length2D();
    outYawDeg   = std::atan2(delta.y, delta.x) * (180.0f / kPi);
    outPitchDeg = std::atan2(delta.z, hyp2D)   * (180.0f / kPi);
}

// DirectX Row-Major 4x4 ViewProjection Matrix WorldToScreen
inline bool WorldToScreen_RowMajor(
    const Vec3& worldPos,
    const float m[16],
    float screenWidth,
    float screenHeight,
    Vec2& outScreen)
{
    float clipX = worldPos.x * m[0]  + worldPos.y * m[1]  + worldPos.z * m[2]  + m[3];
    float clipY = worldPos.x * m[4]  + worldPos.y * m[5]  + worldPos.z * m[6]  + m[7];
    float clipW = worldPos.x * m[12] + worldPos.y * m[13] + worldPos.z * m[14] + m[15];

    if (clipW < 0.01f) return false; // Target is behind the camera

    float ndcX = clipX / clipW;
    float ndcY = clipY / clipW;

    outScreen.x = (screenWidth  * 0.5f) * (1.0f + ndcX);
    outScreen.y = (screenHeight * 0.5f) * (1.0f - ndcY);
    return true;
}

// OpenGL / AssaultCube / Unity Column-Major 4x4 ViewProjection Matrix WorldToScreen
inline bool WorldToScreen_ColMajor(
    const Vec3& worldPos,
    const float m[16],
    float screenWidth,
    float screenHeight,
    Vec2& outScreen)
{
    float clipX = worldPos.x * m[0] + worldPos.y * m[4] + worldPos.z * m[8]  + m[12];
    float clipY = worldPos.x * m[1] + worldPos.y * m[5] + worldPos.z * m[9]  + m[13];
    float clipW = worldPos.x * m[3] + worldPos.y * m[7] + worldPos.z * m[11] + m[15];

    if (clipW < 0.01f) return false;

    float ndcX = clipX / clipW;
    float ndcY = clipY / clipW;

    outScreen.x = (screenWidth  * 0.5f) * (1.0f + ndcX);
    outScreen.y = (screenHeight * 0.5f) * (1.0f - ndcY);
    return true;
}

} // namespace REMath

```

`Tools_and_Scripts/03_Internal_DLL_Skeleton/dllmain_internal_hook.cpp`:

```cpp
// ============================================================================
// [Tool 03] Internal DLL Skeleton & Function Pointer Invoker (C++20)
// ============================================================================
// PURPOSE:
//   A clean Stage 4 Internal DLL template (`DllMain` + worker thread) that can
//   be injected into `lab09_internal_hook_target.exe` (or any offline game).
//   Demonstrates:
//     1. Safe worker thread creation on `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH`
//     2. Resolving an internal engine function via `GetProcAddress` or RVA offset
//     3. Calling `Engine_SpawnMythicChest(0x1337BEEF)` directly from inside the
//        target process's memory space!
//     4. Cleanly unloading the DLL when pressing `[END]`.
// ============================================================================

#ifdef _WIN32
#include <windows.h>
#include <iostream>
#include <cstdint>

using fnSpawnMythicChest = void(*)(uint32_t magicKey);

DWORD WINAPI InternalWorkerThread(LPVOID hModule) {
    std::cout << "\n[+] [INTERNAL DLL] Injected successfully into PID " << GetCurrentProcessId() << "!\n";

    HMODULE hMainExe = GetModuleHandleA(nullptr);
    std::cout << "[+] [INTERNAL DLL] Main Module Base Address: 0x"
              << std::hex << reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(hMainExe) << std::dec << "\n";

    // Look up Lab 09's exported internal function (or replace with Base + RVA offset!)
    auto pSpawnChest = reinterpret_cast<fnSpawnMythicChest>(
        GetProcAddress(hMainExe, "Engine_SpawnMythicChest")
    );

    if (pSpawnChest) {
        std::cout << "[+] [INTERNAL DLL] Located Engine_SpawnMythicChest at 0x"
                  << std::hex << reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(pSpawnChest) << std::dec << "\n";
        std::cout << "[*] [INTERNAL DLL] Invoking Engine_SpawnMythicChest(0x1337BEEF)...\n";
        pSpawnChest(0x1337BEEF);
    } else {
        std::cout << "[!] [INTERNAL DLL] Export not found. In stripped games, use:\n";
        std::cout << "    auto fn = reinterpret_cast<fnType>(moduleBase + 0xRVA);\n";
    }

    FreeLibraryAndExitThread(static_cast<HMODULE>(hModule), 0);
    return 0;
}

BOOL APIENTRY DllMain(HMODULE hModule, DWORD ul_reason_for_call, LPVOID lpReserved) {
    (void)lpReserved;
    if (ul_reason_for_call == DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH) {
        DisableThreadLibraryCalls(hModule);
        HANDLE hThread = CreateThread(nullptr, 0, InternalWorkerThread, hModule, 0, nullptr);
        if (hThread) CloseHandle(hThread);
    }
    return TRUE;
}
#endif

`Tools_and_Scripts/04_Python_RE_Toolkit/crackme_solvers_and_z3_lab.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
============================================================================
[Tool 04-B] Crackme Solver Workbench: Keygen, License Forger, VM & Z3 Lab
============================================================================
PURPOSE:
  Interactive Python companion script for `Crackmes_and_CTF/`:
    1. Keygen solver for Crackme 02 (Mathematical Serial Lock)
    2. Bitwise ROL8/ROR8 inverter for Crackme 03
    3. Binary `license.dat` struct generator for Crackme 04 (`struct.pack`)
    4. Custom Stack VM Disassembler for Crackme 06
    5. Symbolic Constraint Solver (Z3 / Pure Python fallback) for Crackme 08
============================================================================
"""

import struct


def ror8(val: int, shift: int) -> int:
    shift &= 7
    return ((val >> shift) | (val << (8 - shift))) & 0xFF


def solve_crackme02_keygen():
    print("\n--- [Crackme 02 Keygen: Mathematical Serial Lock] ---")
    found = 0
    for a in range(1000, 10000):
        b = 10000 - a
        if 1000 <= b <= 9999 and (a ^ b) == 9984:
            c = ((a * 3) + (b ^ 0x1234)) % 9000 + 1000
            print(f"  [+] Valid Serial #{found + 1}: {a:04d}-{b:04d}-{c:04d}")
            found += 1
            if found >= 4:
                break


def solve_crackme03_cipher():
    print("\n--- [Crackme 03 Decryptor: ROR8 + XOR Loop Inversion] ---")
    encrypted = [
        0xC0, 0x41, 0xE1, 0xC1, 0xF9, 0x71, 0x0E, 0xA6, 0x9E, 0xAE,
        0x1F, 0x7F, 0x5F, 0xC7, 0xAF, 0x84, 0xF4, 0x74, 0x0F, 0x37,
    ]
    chars = []
    for i, b in enumerate(encrypted):
        unrotated = ror8(b, 3)
        plain = unrotated ^ ((0x5A + i * 7) & 0xFF)
        chars.append(chr(plain))
    print(f"  [+] Recovered 20-Char Rune: {''.join(chars)}")


def forge_crackme04_license(output_path: str = "license.dat"):
    print("\n--- [Crackme 04 Forger: Binary `license.dat` Struct] ---")
    magic = 0x56414544        # "DEAV"
    version = 0x0104          # v1.4
    badge = 0x0777            # Tier badge
    citizen_id = 133777       # >= 100,000
    flags = 0x8000002A        # Required feature bitmask
    crc = (magic ^ (citizen_id & 0xFFFFFFFF) ^ flags ^ 0xA5A5A5A5) & 0xFFFFFFFF

    payload = struct.pack("<IHHQII", magic, version, badge, citizen_id, flags, crc)
    with open(output_path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(payload)
    print(f"  [+] Wrote {len(payload)}-byte binary license to `{output_path}` (CRC: 0x{crc:08X})")


def disassemble_crackme06_vm():
    print("\n--- [Crackme 06 Disassembler: Custom Stack VM Bytecode] ---")
    bytecode = [
        0x02, 0x00, 0x01, 0x12, 0x11, 0x01, 0x05, 0x10, 0x01, 0x49, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x01, 0x01, 0x23, 0x11, 0x01, 0x07, 0x10, 0x01, 0x75, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x02, 0x01, 0x34, 0x11, 0x01, 0x09, 0x10, 0x01, 0x74, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x03, 0x01, 0x45, 0x11, 0x01, 0x0B, 0x10, 0x01, 0x19, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x04, 0x01, 0x56, 0x11, 0x01, 0x0D, 0x10, 0x01, 0x2C, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x05, 0x01, 0x67, 0x11, 0x01, 0x0F, 0x10, 0x01, 0x38, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x06, 0x01, 0x78, 0x11, 0x01, 0x11, 0x10, 0x01, 0x50, 0x20,
        0x02, 0x07, 0x01, 0x19, 0x11, 0x01, 0x03, 0x10, 0x01, 0x3B, 0x20,
        0xFF,
    ]
    recovered = []
    for offset in range(0, 88, 11):
        idx = bytecode[offset + 1]
        xor_k = bytecode[offset + 3]
        add_k = bytecode[offset + 6]
        target = bytecode[offset + 9]
        ch = ((target - add_k) & 0xFF) ^ xor_k
        recovered.append(chr(ch))
        print(f"  [VIP 0x{offset:02X}] (input[{idx}] ^ 0x{xor_k:02X}) + 0x{add_k:02X} == 0x{target:02X}  => '{chr(ch)}'")
    print(f"  [+] Solved VM Password: {''.join(recovered)}")


def solve_crackme08_symbolic():
    print("\n--- [Crackme 08 SMT Constraint Solver] ---")
    for b0 in range(32, 127):
        b1 = 141 - b0
        if (b0 ^ b1) != 105:
            continue
        b2 = 44 + b1
        b3 = 172 - b2
        b4 = b3 ^ 12
        b5 = 2 * b4 - 59
        b6 = 140 - b5
        if (b5 ^ b6) != 2:
            continue
        b7 = b6 - 36
        if b0 + b4 + b7 == 188:
            key = "".join(chr(x) for x in (b0, b1, b2, b3, b4, b5, b6, b7))
            print(f"  [+] Unique SMT Model Found: {key}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("====================================================================")
    print("  REVERSE ENGINEERING JOURNEY - PYTHON SOLVER WORKBENCH")
    print("====================================================================")
    solve_crackme02_keygen()
    solve_crackme03_cipher()
    forge_crackme04_license()
    disassemble_crackme06_vm()
    solve_crackme08_symbolic()
